Token rate and segmentation: coarser cadences expand headroom at fixed FLOPs---SyllableLM operates at 5.0--8.33 Hz with ~30$\times$ less LM training compute than AudioLM and 4--4.5$\times$ faster inference at equal LM size while maintaining competitive semantics \cite{baade2024syllablelmlearningcoarsesemanticunitsspe}; ultra--low-rate codecs remain generation-friendly when paired with text-aware decoders, with 10--32 diffusion steps forming an efficiency sweet spot and a favorable reconstruction--generation gap for SLMs \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Backbone capacity and sequence compression: larger decoders consistently improve recognition in Encoder--Adaptor--LLM pipelines (e.g., Qwen2.5-7B > Qwen2-1.5B; Gemma3-12B > Qwen2.5-7B with identical front ends), while projector pooling/splicing (4:1--5:1, 20$\rightarrow$40 ms) halves sequence length with negligible loss \cite{li2025transsionmultilingualspeechrecognitionsy,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. Task asymmetry: scaling disproportionately benefits generative targets---TTS/VC improves faster than ASR as decoder size grows \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}, and discrete TTS dWER drops markedly from ~389M to ~1B parameters---yet cross-domain generalization remains uneven \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}.

In-context learning in SLMs often needs meta-training or prompt-tuning; scale alone is insufficient \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Data scale and token-space construction: interleaved corpora built directly in token space yield monotonic gains from 0$\rightarrow$100B$\rightarrow$200B$\rightarrow$600B tokens and support the $\approx$12.5 Hz operating point for both training and streaming \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.

Capacity planning follows: pick cadence near the knee using prosody-sensitivity and STAB diagnostics ($\approx$12.5 Hz for semantic/mixed tokens), or, at 5--8.33 Hz, budget stronger text-aware decoders plus explicit silence/syllable modeling \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec}. In Encoder--Adaptor--LLM systems, prioritize robust speech backbones with lightweight adapters and reduce sequence length via projector pooling or frame splicing (4--5$\times$ to 25/12.5/6.25 Hz); prefer LM-aligned tokenizers (LM-SPT) and adapt by language to avoid semantic dilution at low rates \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}. Allocate compute to model size over extra tokens; interleaved SLMs scale more efficiently than textless SLMs and show slower linguistic scaling than text LMs, making ASR gains hinge on front-end quality and tokenization choices \cite{cuervo2024scalingpropertiesspeechlanguagemodels,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Comprehensive evaluation should cover multilingual SLU (IndicSUPERB), robustness/knowledge under varied audio (VoxEval), and tokenizer diagnostics (STAB, fastabx) \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.

In Encoder--Adaptor--LLM systems, prioritize robust speech backbones with lightweight adapters and reduce sequence length via projector pooling or frame splicing (4--5$\times$ to 25/12.5/6.25 Hz); prefer LM-aligned tokenizers (LM-SPT) and adapt by language to avoid semantic dilution at low rates \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}. Allocate compute to model size over extra tokens; interleaved SLMs scale more efficiently than textless SLMs and show slower linguistic scaling than text LMs, making ASR gains hinge on front-end quality and tokenization choices \cite{cuervo2024scalingpropertiesspeechlanguagemodels,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,kando2025exploringeffectsegmentationvocabularysiz,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}. Tune decoding temperatures to the interleaving policy \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}, use diffusion/flow decoders with 10--32 steps for ultra--low-rate synthesis while tracking the reconstruction--generation gap \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}, scale interleaved corpora in token space for monotonic returns \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}, and include prompt-tuning or meta-training to elicit ICL-like behaviors \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Comprehensive evaluation should cover multilingual SLU (IndicSUPERB), robustness/knowledge under varied audio (VoxEval), and tokenizer diagnostics (STAB, fastabx) \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}.

Stage 0 (interface and rate control) fixes tokenizer/codec, cadence, bitrate/codebooks, and inversion targets using STAB-style metrics, while mitigating discrete inconsistency for stable conditioning; ultra-low-rate diffusion codecs and VQ-MAE-AV represent efficiency and coverage extremes \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,liu2024analyzingmitigatinginconsistencydiscrete,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Stage 1 (alignment) follows connector-first bridges---LM-aware tokens (LAST, LM-SPT) or Whisper$\rightarrow$LLM projectors with progressive unfreezing only as needed \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}---or continued pretraining with shared-vocabulary interleaving at $\approx$12.5 Hz that scales more efficiently than textless SLMs while preserving knowledge \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,nachmani2024spokenquestionansweringspeechcontinuatio}; affect-first alignment can precede paralinguistic grounding (BLSP-Emo) \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}. Stage 2 consolidates with supervised/instruction data---real and synthetic pairs, mined speech, language-specific tuning---and can add auxiliary SER heads for affect; textless SLMs benefit from prompt-tuning warmups with few-shot demonstrations, and decoder-only ASR gains from interleaved LM-only updates plus joint CTC--decoder training \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,noroozi2024instructiondatagenerationunsupervisedada,lu2025desta2developinginstructionfollowingspee,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla,tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}. Stage 3 specializes for duplex/streaming via IRQ prediction, barge-in handling, and streaming curricula that interleave partial transcripts/plans before speech emission, and integrates SpeechRAG for listen-and-retrieve \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou}. Stage 4 applies preference learning/RL with structural constraints (e.g., KL/DPO proximity, structural tokens, optimal transport) and second-pass rescoring with contextual biasing, few-shot prompts, and checkpoint averaging, achieving sizable WER gains \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext,sun2023contextualbiasingnamedentitieslargelangu}.

A stable speech--LM contract uses invariant interfaces and alignment-aware compression: text-aligned discrete semantic tokens (LM-SPT) or a fixed-geometry continuous token space (continuous tokens/flow matching) predicted by the LM, with learned semantic reduction rather than rigid pooling; reconstruction matches a frozen ASR encoder at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz to preserve lexical anchors and paralinguistics for low-latency streaming and improved S2T/TTS \cite{jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,yuan2024continuousspeechtokensmakesllmsrobustmul}. Interfaces are enforced either by adaptor-first bridges with uniform 4$\times$ subsampling into the LLM embedding space \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}, shared-vocabulary SLMs with a unified discrete inventory at 12.5 Hz \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}, or ultra-low-rate BSQ codecs at 6.25 Hz consumed by AR/masked decoders \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}; RepCodec offers information-rich semantic tokens from HuBERT/data2vec for seamless integration \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}.

Compression should not erode semantics: fixing 12.5 Hz during interleaved pretraining standardizes targets and shortens sequences, while 4$\times$ subsampling reduces prefilling but motivates alignment-preserving compression; at 6.25 Hz, text-aware decoding closes reconstruction--generation gaps and preserves intelligibility/identity when tokenizer and decoder are co-designed, with OT-based regularization validated by STAB/IndicSUPERB \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Lexical anchoring holds under shared 12.5 Hz vocabularies and conservative continuous bridges; ultra-low-rate BSQ with text-aware decoding sustains cross-lingual WER \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Continuous tokenizers (Cont-SPT) often capture finer semantics/robustness than discrete tokens; decoupled tokenization with multi-token prediction improves information density and speed, while XY-Tokenizer tightens text alignment and acoustic fidelity with speaker-aware generation \cite{li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,gong2025xytokenizermitigatingsemanticacousticcon,fan2025whatmakesgoodspeechtokenizerllmcentricge,wang2024comparativestudydiscretespeechtokenssema}. A pragmatic blueprint fixes one interface end-to-end (discrete at a fixed Hz or continuous projection), compresses on the perception path, avoids shifting downstream contracts, audits side-effects (rates, downsampling, alignment-guided vs random), and reports vocabulary/rate consistency across pretraining and fine-tuning \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,li2025continuousspeechtokenizertext,verma2024whispergpthybridrepresentationaudiolarge,wang2024c3llmconditionalmultimodalcontentgenerat}.

A rate- and schedule-aware protocol should standardize interfaces (unit tokenizers; SSR-style segmentation/compression aligned to text tokens), fix compute budgets, and quantify alignment, retention, robustness, and forgetting. Evaluation spans zero-shot cross-modal transfer, spoken QA/knowledge robustness under varied audio (VoxEval), and scaling behavior of interleaved SLMs that favor model size over token count while leveraging synthetic data and low-budget SSL pretraining \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,tan2025ssralignmentawaremodalityconnectorspeech,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste}. Core SLU metrics (ASR, SV/SID, LID, QbE, KWS) should be paired with acoustic slices (noise, emotion, speaker, room), speech--text alignment probes, and tokenizer consistency analyses \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Recognition scoring uses IndicSUPERB and STAB with consistent toolchains, fixed decoding, and checkpoint averaging \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}; duplex/TTS fidelity is measured by Whisper-v3 WER on LibriTTS-testsetB without interruptions and turn-taking F1 within [0,1.0 s] windows \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}.

Tokenizer robustness should connect STAB axes---speaker/context/language invariance, pitch/speed/noise robustness, compressibility, vocabulary utilization---to downstream trends with multi-seed CIs \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. For AV emotion, ablations on fusion (self vs cross-attention), loss mixtures, SER heads (attention pooling, mean, Query2Emo), modality contributions, token sizes, and encoder depth consistently favor cross-attention fusion and Query2Emo with both VQ pretraining and encoder fine-tuning \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Expressive SLMs such as ProsodyLM, GOAT-SLM, and SeamlessExpressiveLM aim to co-model lexical content with prosody, identity, and affect under bitrate/latency budgets, yet capability probes still surface gaps: BoSS finds weak emotion alignment and limited age-aware adaptation in omni/SLM stacks, and TTS comparisons show an expressivity--identity trade-off---Bark increases F0/rate variance but degrades speaker stability relative to VITS, with only modest gains from scaling \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}. We distill these themes in the hierarchical map shown in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_6}, which organizes the space along three top-level branches: (1) factorized architectures that preserve global style while controlling local, word-aligned prosody; (2) lightweight, streamable control surfaces and signal-aligned supervision---including adapters, interpretable heads, audio--visual fusion, and phonetic--acoustic tokenizers---that disentangle affect from identity; and (3) practical scaling with frozen foundations, interleaved multitask training, new resources/diagnostics, and rate-aware decoding that ties expressivity to lexical timing. The structure foregrounds the expressivity--identity trade-off, promotes disentangled semantic/acoustic pathways, and situates training/evaluation recipes with respect to BoSS-style capability gaps.

Aligned with branch (2), control surfaces should be lightweight and streamable. Short enrollment prompts provide global levers for identity/emotion/intonation at ultra-low token rates but risk identity drift as expressivity rises \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}. Label-efficient adapters and interpretable heads (e.g., BLSP-Emo, EMOVA, Dual-Adapter SLMs) improve affect control, while audio--visual fusion helps when acoustics alone are ambiguous \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,chen2025emovaempoweringlanguagemodelsseehearspea,wang2025dualinformationspeechlanguagemodelsemoti}. Signal-aligned supervision and light bridges between pretrained speech and text foundations are effective: time-stamped categorical/dimensional labels and phonetic-acoustic tokenizers (PAST) strengthen representations, and thin projectors/adapters add $\approx$1% parameters yet yield strong ASR/AST, contextual biasing, and zero-shot instruction following while preserving affect cues in multilingual settings \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}. Identity remains fragile at low bitrates---BoSS highlights verification/diarization weaknesses---motivating disentangled channels, explicit identity heads, and decoder regularization when targeting high prosodic variance \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech,wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.

Multi-stream and RVQ-aware designs disentangle content from style and lower next-token entropy on the semantic path. A unified tokenizer that concentrates linguistic content in RVQ-1 and paralinguistics in later RVQ layers achieves low WER with high speaker similarity; mixed local tokens plus a compact global style vector similarly preserve timbre; audio--visual discrete tokens add prosody--face coupling and protect emotion cues under masking \cite{zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar,messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage,sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis}. Direct manipulation unlocks precise trade-offs and edits. Stream-ID tokens select residual codec streams to dial prosodic richness against latency/bandwidth, source-style prompts preserve timbre across S2ST, decoder temperature in flow-matching acts as a global expressivity knob, and role prompts can be composed with attribute tags and acoustic prompts for persona synthesis; when time-stamped SER exists, metadata can bias affect regionally if conflicts are mitigated \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext,yuan2024continuousspeechtokensmakesllmsrobustmul,wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}. Fine-grained word-synchronous controls---pause insertion at punctuation, duration adjustments via alignment ratios, F0 transfer with normalization/interpolation---enable localized edits under strict budgets and complement global prompting \cite{huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}. For deployment, IndicSUPERB facilitates multilingual SLU, and STITCH lets models interleave silent reasoning with speech to match latency while improving math reasoning \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}. Evaluations should couple control-compliance with semantic integrity---BLEU/BERTScore for S2ST under fixed prompt ratios/stream-IDs, instruction-only empathy with alignment-matched prompts, word-aligned metrics for pause/duration/F0 edits---and, under ultra-low rates, audits of reconstruction--generation gaps while sweeping prompt conditioning and decoder temperature \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu,huang2023holisticcascadesystembenchmarkhumanevalu}.

\noindent Real-time agents must coordinate perception, planning, and synthesis across concurrent input/output channels under strict latency, bandwidth, and compute limits. A systems-first view treats latency as the primary constraint: decompose end-to-end delay into capture, buffering, perception/prefill, time-to-first-audio, and steady-state generation; then optimize three levers---(i) chunking and read--write schedules that cap look-ahead while preserving coherence, (ii) low-rate, streamable token interfaces that shrink conditioning length and bandwidth, and (iii) budgeted inference that cuts prefill and decoding cost without sacrificing intelligibility or style. These principles structure the subsequent subsections on latency-aware design, streaming policies, duplex turn-taking, and architectures.

\noindent Real-time agents must coordinate perception, planning, and synthesis across concurrent input/output channels under strict latency, bandwidth, and compute limits. A systems-first view treats latency as the primary constraint: decompose end-to-end delay into capture, buffering, perception/prefill, time-to-first-audio, and steady-state generation; then optimize three levers---(i) chunking and read--write schedules that cap look-ahead while preserving coherence, (ii) low-rate, streamable token interfaces that shrink conditioning length and bandwidth, and (iii) budgeted inference that cuts prefill and decoding cost without sacrificing intelligibility or style. These principles structure the subsequent subsections on latency-aware design, streaming policies, duplex turn-taking, and architectures.

Chunking, buffering, and read--write schedules. With causal tokenizers, short interleaved cycles deliver fast onset: GLM-4-Voice starts speaking after $\approx$10 speech tokens with a text-first plan; aligning assistant text insertion to the first speech chunk index k_start and setting τ_SC=5 at 12.5 Hz ($\approx$400 ms) balances semantic planning and responsiveness in duplex chat \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. For SimulST, fixed word-lag k with 320--640 ms chunks exposes latency--quality frontiers, while semantic truncation bounds context for long streams \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Warning 8 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 844: Wrong length of dash may have been used.
First-chunk policies and reporting. Deterministic read--write alternation with short text plans and small speech blocks minimizes time-to-first-audio; GLM-4-Voice's $\approx$10-token onset and τ_SC=5 at 12.5 Hz are concrete targets \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. Report wall-clock effects and schedules jointly: fixed word-lag k and chunk sizes in SimulST \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}; token-to-speech thresholds and interleaving ratios in duplex chat \cite{zeng2024glm4voicetowardsintelligenthumanlikeends}; whether CTC sparsity is used \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}; and, for generation, token rate (12.5 vs 6.25 Hz), bitrate, decoder family, and diffusion steps, noting that few-step schedules and ultra-low-rate tokens markedly lower RTF and bandwidth.

Streaming policies decide when to read audio and write tokens, coupling encoder chunking, tokenizer cadence, and decoder scheduling. Four recurring strategies---wait-k, read--write alternation, incremental decoding with request-more-audio control, and duration-synchronous generation---integrate naturally with streamable encoders (Emformer, HuBERT/data2vec), fixed-rate discrete tokenizers (e.g., RepCodec, SpeechTokenizer), and unified SLM decoders \cite{huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,zhang2024speechtokenizerunifiedspeechtokenizerlar}.

Warning 36 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 854: You should put a space in front of parenthesis.
Wait-k and prefix-to-prefix. BESTOW samples K∼U(3,12) during training and sets task-specific K at inference (e.g., K=6 for ASR), combining look-ahead L=4 with pre-decision steps P=8 and supporting uni/bidirectional streaming to hit latency targets \cite{chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem}. SimulS2S-LLM derives a test-time wait-k from CIF prompt length, stabilizing prefix decoding via frame-synchronous CTC, incremental beam search, and shallow-fused n-gram LM \cite{deng2025simuls2sllmunlockingsimultaneousinferenc}. In shared tasks, EOS-to-request-more-audio realizes incremental reading with a latency multiplier for soft wait-k; StreamUni further gates generation by transcription growth and prunes served history via semantic truncation \cite{ouyang2025cmusiwslt2025simultaneousspeechtranslati,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Read--write alternation. In plan-before-speak assistants, emission after every R text tokens yields deterministic cycles and prompt audio onset; first-chunk latency decomposes as LLM(R)+TTS(W)+FlowMatching(W)+Vocoder(2W), exposing tunable trade-offs over R and W \cite{fang2025llamaomni2llmbasedrealtimespokenchatbota}. StreamUni complements these cycles with transcription-driven gates and semantic truncation and reports AL/LAAL/StreamLAAL using mWERSegmenter \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Duration-synchronous generation. SyncSpeech aligns emission to predicted durations with look-ahead q=1, enabling true online dual-stream decoding; unlike full-context NAR flow/diffusion, it supports continuous generation under read--write/wait-k \cite{sheng2025syncspeechlowlatencyefficientdualstreamt}.

Warning 8 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 878: Wrong length of dash may have been used.
Policy learning vs inference-time control. Next-token--level supervision internalizes turn-taking beyond thresholds. NTPP jointly predicts next-token pairs to model overlaps, backchannels, and interruptions without external VAD; an interruption suite with 400 synthetic scenarios evaluates ``speak-up'' and interruption responses, and channel swapping validates speaker independence \cite{wang2025ntppgenerativespeechlanguagemodelingdual}. TurnGuide inserts brief plan-first text just before assistant speech, preserving duplex dynamics and improving coherence, with Candor correlations on IPU/pause/gap/overlap comparable or superior to token-synchronous baselines \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}.

Warning 8 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 880: Wrong length of dash may have been used.
Evaluation methodology and training signals. Use pyannote.audio VAD to compute time-normalized per-channel IPU/pause/gap/overlap counts/durations; closeness to human distributions diagnoses scheduling quality \cite{lu2025slideintegratingspeechlanguagemodelllmsp}. For interruptions, report barge-in success, end-to-end interruption latency, and interactivity F1 under clean/noisy regimes, apply standardized false-alarm rules, stress-test speaker independence via channel swapping, and avoid contaminated test sets \cite{maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,wang2025ntppgenerativespeechlanguagemodelingdual}. Style--role suites (e.g., StyleSet) cover sequential turn-taking but not overlap/barge-in; duplex-native evaluations and turn-level correlations on conversational corpora (e.g., Candor) fill the gap \cite{chiang2025audioawarelargelanguagemodelsasjudgesspe,cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. Targeted data---barge-in augmentation (truncate speech, insert $\approx$0.64 s silence), synthetic interruption suites, inference-only bootstraps (end-of-turn thresholds, silence counters)---combines with integrated policies (overlap caps, IRQ heads, token-pair objectives) and direct duplex S2S fusion to yield natural alternation, timely backchannels, and reliable barge-in under noise with modest data \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,wang2025dualspeechlmtowardsunifiedspeechundersta}. Operational reporting should include dataset provenance/scale (e.g., IndicSUPERB 1,684 h/12 languages), SLU coverage, tokenizer quality (STAB), and duplex performance under SALMon-style perturbations with contamination-checked holds \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,yang2024buildingtaiwanesemandarinspokenlanguagem}.

Turn-aware I/O interleaving. TurnGuide interleaves user/assistant channels at the chunk level (τ_SC=5 at 12.5 Hz) with UID/AID markers, preserving per-channel timing and enabling barge-in/overlap; assistant turns align with pyannote VAD, Whisper timestamps, and punctuation inside a GLM-4-Voice backbone \cite{cui2025thinkbeforeyoutalkenhancingmeaningfuldia}. Chunk-level alternation with token-level read--write ratios bounds first-audio while reusing a single KV cache.

This section consolidates datasets, benchmarks, and task formulations spanning ASR/SLU, S2TT/S2ST, TTS/resynthesis, paralinguistics, audio--visual speech, and duplex dialogue. We systematize corpora by modality, domain, and language coverage; outline common construction/split conventions and tokenization settings; map tasks to datasets/metrics; and compile benchmark suites with multilingual, robustness, streaming, and reproducibility considerations \cite{fily2024establishingdegreesclosenessbetweenaudio,futeral2025madspeechmeasuresacousticdiversity,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}.

Modality and domain. Transcript-free SpeechRAG aligns speech--text embeddings for direct retrieval and audio-conditioned generation, mitigating ASR error propagation; TWIST and tokenizer progress (PAST, LM-SPT, LAST) improve recognition/generation and enable low-rate streaming \cite{min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,hassid2024textuallypretrainedspeechlanguagemodels,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization}.

Construction and splits. Standard ASR uses LibriSpeech dev/test-clean; contamination in LibriSpeech/Common Voice motivates audited holds; 16 kHz is common for ASR/tokenizers, 24 kHz for TTS/codecs; token frame rate governs information retention and varies by language \cite{tseng2025evaluationllmsspeechisoftenflawedtestset,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy,li2024investigatingneuralaudiocodecsspeechlang,ye2024codecdoesmatterexploringsemanticshortcom,li2024effectivenessacousticbpedecoderonlytts}. Provenance, licensing, privacy. LLaSO releases alignment/instruction pairs and a reference model; open S2S/VoiceTextBlender efforts release code/data/weights for augmentation and alignment \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere,peng2025voicetextblenderaugmentinglargelanguagem}. Reproducible scoring and protocol detail. Recommended checklist. Use IndicSUPERB, SALMon, and VoxEval as standard suites; report dataset scale and language coverage; include FBANK and SSL finetunes; evaluate ASR accuracy, acoustic consistency, speaker/language ID, KWS, robustness, and reasoning. Publish stratified, voice-/speaker-disjoint splits and construction scripts; standardize sample rates/metadata; disclose real/synthetic ratios and scope limits; quantify/mitigate test overlap with decontaminated results and standardized tokenizer assessments (STAB) \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. For streaming/rate-aware models, report token frame rate (25/12.5/6.25 Hz), bitrate, synthesis backend and steps, latency metrics alongside quality, and read--write schedules \cite{zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}. When assets cannot be released, provide risk rationales, watermarking, and evaluator calibration. These practices support fair, reproducible comparisons as SLMs adopt LM-aware/aligned tokenizers (LAST, LM-SPT), phonetic--acoustic designs (PAST), and higher-fidelity codecs (RepCodec) \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz}.

  \item Intelligibility proxies and diagnostics. Report WER/CER (with Sub/Del/Ins) using a fixed recognizer/decoding policy; Whisper-large-v3 WER on generated audio is common in duplex settings, but proxy bias rises at high WER where ASR-free retrieval (SpeechRAG) is preferable \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. We adopt a structured view of latency evaluation that is summarized by the hierarchical map in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_9}. At the top level, the map organizes (i) core latency definitions and protocols---RTF, TTFT, streaming lag, and duplex IRQ---anchored to shared long-audio workloads; (ii) compute accounting and path-wise latency decomposition spanning encoder buffering, tokenization/adapters, LLM prefill/decoding, and token-to-waveform synthesis, including policy and token-rate effects; and (iii) reporting fields and normalization guidance, covering required fields and duplex sheets, acoustic standardization, dataset/split and tokenization-alignment controls, quality--latency curves, and full compute/time disclosure. Citations throughout indicate exemplar methods and benchmarks supporting each element. \subsection{Bandwidth and edge--server trade-offs} \label{subsec:bandwidthandedge--servertrade-offs}  
                              ^^
Warning 8 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 1247: Wrong length of dash may have been used.
Deployments must balance link bitrate, VRAM/KV growth, and server FLOPs while supporting duplex, low-latency interaction. Low-rate token streams are viable: semantic-conditioned DiffSoundStream matches SoundStream quality at $\approx$50 tps with four diffusion steps \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}; textless streaming S2ST achieves strong BLEU/latency/BLASER-2.0 by emitting discrete tokens end-to-end \cite{zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant}. XY-Tokenizer improves the semantic--acoustic trade-off with high speaker similarity at comparable rates \cite{gong2025xytokenizermitigatingsemanticacousticcon}, and SALM-Duplex caps downlink near 0.6 kbps with high UTMOS and speaker similarity, establishing a sub-kbps working point; $\approx$0.3 kbps modes remain intelligible for poor connectivity \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,xue2025hhcodechighcompressionfidelitydiscretene}. A practical ladder is 0.3$\rightarrow$0.6$\rightarrow$1.24 kbps, with language-dependent adjustments for prosodic density \cite{onda2025benchmarkingprosodyencodingdiscretespeec,zhang2025impactframeratesspeechtokenizercasestudy}.

Warning 39 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 1249: Double space found.
Separating perception from generation decouples encoder compute from codec bitrate and eases heterogeneous client integration; semantic-conditioned tokenization shifts information into the semantic path to lighten the acoustic stream when both are transmitted \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. On constrained VRAM, Slam proves single-GPU training in ~24 h \cite{maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}; FastAdaSP enables up to 7$\times$ memory reduction and $\approx$1.8$\times$ throughput gains for higher concurrency \cite{lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}. Mixed speech--text decoders fit within $\approx$19 GB VRAM on 8B backbones for on-prem single-GPU serving; quantization (4--8 bit) and expert routing by language/task further reduce VRAM/FLOPs \cite{dao2025ichigomixedmodalearlyfusionrealtimevoice,lu2024fastadaspmultitaskadaptedefficientinfere}. Interleaved speech--text pretraining improves scaling efficiency and shifts optimal spend toward larger models; speech-native RAG bypasses ASR cascades, reducing latency and compounding errors \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,min2025speechretrievalaugmentedgenerationwithou,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,maimon2025slammingtrainingspeechlanguagemodelonegp}.

- Optimal Transport Regularization narrows the speech--text gap and improves multilingual generalization with a lightweight, label-free term; a two-stage schedule (2+3 epochs; AdamW 1e-4$\rightarrow$1e-6; λ_spr=0.1) is reproducible \cite{xu2025optimaltransportregularizationspeechtext}.

These practices---paired with interleaved speech--text scaling that prioritizes model size, LM-aware tokenization (LAST/LM-SPT/PAST/dMel), and bandwidth-aware streaming policies---enable reproducible, compute-light development of duplex, barge-in-capable agents under tight latency and memory budgets \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang}. Read together with the high-level structure in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_10}, the section provides both a compact roadmap and the concrete, cited steps needed for faithful reproduction and fair comparison.

Tokenization and codec targets. NAST produces local units plus a global residual stable under time-stretch, pitch-shift, reverberation, and noise, disentangling style \cite{messica2024nastnoiseawarespeechtokenizationlanguage}. USM-based tokenizers validated with STAB---and boosted by ASR losses---show content-oriented supervision increases invariance \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}. dMel tokens align to perceptual time--frequency structure, improving robustness to music/background speech at comparable bitrates over HuBERT-k-means and SpeechTokenizer; self-segmented syllable-synchronous embeddings provide rate-controlled, cross-lingual stability \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple}.

Warning 8 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 1372: Wrong length of dash may have been used.
The 5C lens---Code, Config, Compute, Corpora, Comparison---specifies the open artifacts, rate-/latency-aware settings, and disclosure norms required for reproducible and cross-lab comparable speech--AI. As visualized in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_12}, the overview is organized into three complementary pillars: (i) open stack exemplars (tokenizers/codecs and corpora), (ii) standardized evaluation anchors (low-cost, rate-/robustness-aware diagnostics), and (iii) benchmark-card disclosures (coverage, conditions, comparability, and compute/cost reporting). Together, these pillars link open, inspectable pipelines to comparable cross-lab evaluation via explicit, auditable reporting. It emphasizes end-to-end, inspectable stacks spanning tokenizers/codecs and LM bridges, interleaved SLMs, decoders/vocoders, and a standardized evaluation harness with dataset hygiene and statistical testing, exemplified by streamable dMel discretization with parallel encode/decode, RepCodec's high-fidelity semantic tokens, and LLaSO's public Align/Instruct/Eval sets with a 3.8B reference model \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere}.

Warning 8 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 1377: Wrong length of dash may have been used.
Open ecosystems now release code, configs, and checkpoints with pinned evaluators and interfaces. LM-aligned tokenizers/codecs (LAST, RepCodec) retain semantics better than k-means; interleaved SLMs reuse a single LM for speech+text; IndicSUPERB supplies reproducible SLU baselines and leaderboards across 12 languages; toolchains integrate ESPnet/fairseq/NeMo/SpeechBrain/s3prl and log token/frame rates, bitrates, and streaming schedules \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,huang2024repcodecspeechrepresentationcodectokeniz,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Representative releases include: ESPnet recipes for encoders, tokenizers, decoders/vocoders, and decoder-only ASR with optional shallow-fusion LMs \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}; SpeechLM toolkits that standardize data prep, pre-training, inference, and evaluation, with ProsodyLM for word-level prosody tokens and ESPnet-SpeechLM covering 15+ tasks \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,tian2025espnetspeechlmopenspeechlanguagemodeltoo}; LLaSO's public model, datasets, and evaluation suite \cite{sun2025llasofoundationalframeworkreproduciblere}; OpenS2S for streaming, empathetic interleaved decoding \cite{wang2025opens2sadvancingfullyopensourceendempath}; SimulST/duplex SLM repos with fixed interleaving and read--write policies and first-audio latency under SimulEval \cite{har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer,guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,chen2024bestowefficientstreamablespeechlanguagem}; AV tokenizers (VQ-MAE-AV) and streamable phonetic tokenizers (PAST) for controlled fusion under fixed token budgets \cite{sadok2025vectorquantizedmaskedautoencoderaudiovis,har-tuv2025pastphoneticacousticspeechtokenizer}; evaluation harnesses integrating IndicSUPERB, STAB, and fastabx for scalable probes \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}; Encoder--Adaptor--LLM bridges, diarization toolkits, and unified scoring pipelines for replicable ASR/SQA ablations \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}; and community suites expanding discrete-token leaderboards (DASB), speech QA (VoxEval), and audio tasks (Dynamic-SUPERB Phase-2) \cite{mousavi2024dasbdiscreteaudiospeechbenchmark,cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}. Ready-to-run tokenizer training (memory-aware k-means), interleaved speech--text pretraining in token space, and mixed-vocabulary duplex stacks should ship with fixed configs/checkpoints, and STITCH-style think-while-talking experiments must publish prompts, seeds, and chunking/scheduling scripts \cite{chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,dao2025ichigomixedmodalearlyfusionrealtimevoice,chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}.

This section distills open problems and concrete paths for streamable, duplex speech--language systems that retain text competence, scale multilingually, and operate under realistic rate--compute budgets across five axes: interfaces that disentangle lexical content from residual acoustics; long-context memory for multi-turn interaction; low-resource and multilingual generalization; alignment to text knowledge without catastrophic forgetting; and a rate--capacity perspective linking token cadence, per-token entropy, and decoder scheduling with evaluation guardrails.

Warning 8 in /Users/xjp/Desktop/Survey-with-LLMs/Survey-for-survey-review-with-LLMs/SurveyX/sandbox/latex_assembly_demo/compiled_output/survey.tex line 1438: Wrong length of dash may have been used.
Interface choice sets the entropy presented to the semantic planner and the controllability of prosody/speaker identity at a given bitrate, thereby constraining streaming and duplex schedules. Factorized designs split a low-entropy semantic stream from acoustic/codec tokens, enabling conditional decoding that reduces redundancy, preserves controllability at low rates, and supports direct streaming S2ST without ASR$\rightarrow$MT$\rightarrow$TTS cascades or unified LSLMs that interleave segmentation, policy, and translation \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla,zhao2024textlessstreamingspeechtranslationsemant,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. Unified designs remain competitive when decoders are text/prompt-aware and aligned: semantics and speaker/style are pushed into conditioning, permitting ultra-low-rate operation with intelligibility and style retention across ASR, TTS, VC, enhancement, and mixed speech--text generation, albeit with potential residual entanglement inside a single stream \cite{wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz,wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}.

A capability-centric decomposition suggests explicit channels for lexical content (V_L), affect (V_AC), contextual dynamics (V_CD), implicit semantics (V_IS), and non-verbals (e.g., laughter, backchannels) to expose beyond-semantic control \cite{wang2025bossbeyondsemanticspeech}. Mixed tokenizers that condition acoustics on semantics (e.g., FiLM-style conditioning) reduce cross-stream redundancy and enable multi-level, multi-rate hierarchies aligned to linguistic structure and streaming constraints \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}. A pragmatic hybrid keeps lexical progression in a predictable, low-entropy semantic stream and adds a thin, low-bandwidth control plane for style/affect and non-verbals; discrete--continuous mixes and global control vectors instantiate this while preserving editability and duplex responsiveness \cite{yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati}.

Duplex agents must reason over minutes of audio and preserve state across turns while remaining streamable. Current stacks highlight windowing and segmentation limits: decoder-only speech LMs stabilize in-context learning at $\approx$4 demonstrations and prefer fixed-length formatting \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}; Encoder--Adaptor--LLM pipelines commonly bound context to ~30 s segments with overlap, constraining cross-turn dependencies \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}; lightweight history already helps (e.g., two-utterance augmentation improves mid-training WER/CER) \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}. Streaming translation depends on punctuation cues and heuristic boundaries, motivating trainable semantic boundary detectors, adaptive delay policies, and joint speech-encoder--LSLM training \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Community evaluation is beginning to reflect longer-audio and multi-audio protocols \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}. These constraints, and the modular remedies they invite, are synthesized in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_13}, which organizes (i) current streaming limitations (few-shot ICL saturation, segmentation and boundary heuristics), (ii) a duplex-aware memory blueprint (small local windows plus sparse, turn-synchronous summaries with prosody-aware compression and joint encoder--LSLM policy alignment), and (iii) a phased curriculum with evaluation and reporting (fixed-window warmup, boundary/delay supervision, distant supervision, progressive unfreezing, and memory-centric probes/metrics). Taken together, the chart maps constraints to design choices and training--evaluation practices for robust, low-latency duplex dialogue.

Aligned with the memory blueprint highlighted in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_13}, a streaming-/duplex-aware memory maintains (i) a small, fast local context for token-level decisions and (ii) sparse, turn-synchronous summaries in a persistent, channel-tagged store so overlaps/interruptions do not erase state. Summaries should be rate-aware, combining variable-rate compression with anchors that preserve prosody/non-verbals; write/evict/retrieve policies are trained with latency objectives and informed by learned boundaries and adaptive delay \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Mechanistically: derive turn-synchronous anchors from streaming representations; keep local windows fixed-length for stable in-context behavior \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}; smooth boundaries via overlap--add or masked streaming schedules; retrieve conditioned on speaker and turn under duplex constraints; and align memory control with streaming policy via joint encoder--LSLM training \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}. Practical recipes: prepend one--two prior utterances for conversational ASR \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}; for assistants, store channel-tagged turn summaries and retrieve each turn while writing on longer segments but inferring on short windows \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}; for simultaneous translation, replace punctuation triggers with learned boundaries and adaptive delay \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}.

Building on the curriculum and evaluation axis depicted in Figure~\ref{fig:tiny_tree_figure_13}, a phased curriculum introduces memory without destabilizing streaming: start with fixed-length local windows \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}; add supervision for boundary detection and delay control \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}; incorporate distant supervision for memory writes from transcript segmentation or multi-audio alignment \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}; and progressively unfreeze the encoder to manage compute and latency \cite{nguyen2025qwenvsgemmaintegrationwhispercomparative}. Evaluation tracks should include multi-turn instruction tasks with minute-scale memory probes and multi-source reasoning \cite{huang2025dynamicsuperbphase2collaborativelyexpand}, recall--lag curves and learned boundary/delay policies under noise \cite{guo2025streamuniachievingstreamingspeechtransla}, with/without-context ablations reflecting two-utterance gains \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}, and speech in-context scaling beyond four demonstrations with variable-length prompts \cite{hsu2024exploringcontextlearningtextlessspeechla}. Reporting minimums: segment size/overlap; boundary detector and delay policy; memory write/eviction rates, lookup latency, cache hits; duplex channel tagging/conflict resolution; quality--latency deltas with/without memory; SLM initialization/interleaving; compute budget/allocation; synthetic data and TextLM family; continual-learning stages and retention metrics; forgetting mitigation; and reproducibility metadata \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,hsiao2025analyzingmitigationstrategiescatastrophi,chen2023reducingbarriersselfsupervisedlearninghu}.

An actionable blueprint integrates IndicSUPERB for multi-task SLU across 12 Indic languages \cite{entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}; LM-aware tokenization (LAST, LM-SPT) aligning speech tokens to a single LM at 25/12.5/6.25 Hz \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,jo2025lmsptalignedsemanticdistillationspeechto}; VoxEval for spoken QA and robustness; SALMon for acoustic awareness; and fastabx for ABX discriminability \cite{cui2025voxevalbenchmarkingknowledgeunderstandin,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati,poli2025fastabxlibraryefficientcomputationabxdis}. Practical levers include LM-aware tokenization (LAST) and tokenizer diagnostics (STAB) that predict downstream quality \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark}, chunked think-and-talk decoding (STITCH) for silent reasoning at constant latency \cite{chiang2025stitchsimultaneousthinkingtalkingchunked}, and adapter bridges that enable ASR/AST and zero-shot instruction following with frozen backbones \cite{wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat}. Additional tactics include prompt bottlenecks for decoder-only ASR with pseudo-prompt curricula \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}, behavior KL to the base policy \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}, and RL safeguards with KL/hypothesis-similarity constraints when process-based rewards are sparse/ineffective \cite{li2025seewossubmissionmlcslmlessonslearnedspee}. Design pillars: cadence shaping and abstraction alignment to fit R within B at target scale, reducing H through structure-preserving abstraction rather than blunt downsampling; dual-channel apportionment of B across lexical and paralinguistic streams with cross-layer alignment to each stream's entropy budget; and scheduler-level control of bits-per-step (diffusion steps, acoustic depth, multi-token prediction) to keep online R compatible with B under streaming \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. A measurement program should run iso-rate sweeps (vary decoder expressivity/schedule at fixed r), iso-capacity sweeps (vary r at fixed θ/S to locate knees near $\approx$12.5 Hz and validate 6.25 Hz feasibility), and cross-modal competition tests that vary interleaving and size to map interference thresholds; reporting must include R = r$\cdot$H estimates, schedule descriptors, and downstream quality/latency to place systems on a shared rate--capacity plot \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,yang2025diffsoundstreamefficientspeechtokenizati,wang2025tadicodectextawarediffusionspeechtokeniz}. Actionable defaults: 12.5 Hz for mixed streams with entropy reduction via segmental abstraction/quantization; 6.25 Hz for large/hybrid stacks when tokens are generation-aligned and decoders operate at 10--32 diffusion steps; capacity apportioned across lexical/acoustic channels with abstractions aligned accordingly, using schedule-level knobs to maintain streaming compliance.

Choices in interfaces, tokenization, decoders, alignment curricula, and evaluation co-determine quality, latency, robustness, and validity. Thin adapters (~1%/156M) over frozen speech/text foundations bridge modalities and enable zero-shot instruction following; LM-aware tokenization narrows tokenizer--LM mismatch so a single LM handles speech and text; interleaved SLMs initialized from TextLMs scale compute-efficiently relative to textless models, though multilingual disparities remain on IndicSUPERB and demand co-design and smarter compute allocation \cite{maimon2025scalinganalysisinterleavedspeechtextlang,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2023slmbridgethingapbetweenspeechtextfoundat,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf}. Limitations include: decoder-only ASR sensitivity to CTC-prompt design and capacity ceilings \cite{tsunoo2024decoderonlyarchitecturespeechrecognition}; end-to-end S2ST with a single decoder lagging encoder--decoder S2ST and constrained by channel scheduling \cite{peng2024mslms2stmultitaskspeechlanguagemodeltext}; unified decoders trailing task experts in ASR/enhancement and being data-hungry \cite{wu2024speechcomposerunifyingmultiplespeechtask}; duplex LMs degrading under noise/speaker mismatch without echo cancellation/diarization and lacking standardized full-duplex benchmarks \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}; empathy/capability evaluations relying on synthetic or single-turn judgments \cite{wang2024blspemotowardsempatheticlargespeechlangu}; SLM-driven TTS improving prosodic diversity but lagging on intelligibility/speaker consistency and susceptible to ASR-derived biases \cite{wang2024evaluatingtextspeechsynthesislargediscre}.

Safety claims should be auditable and comparable. An evaluation card specifies robustness assumptions (acoustic confounds, adversarial budgets), metrics (Safety/Relevance/Helpfulness, JSR, SPR/SNR), benchmarks (IndicSUPERB, STAB, SALMon), judges (human and calibrated ALLMs), and governance (data provenance, contributor coverage, code/data release) \cite{vashishth2024stabspeechtokenizerassessmentbenchmark,entry2025indicsuperbspeechprocessinguniversalperf,maimon2025salmonsuiteacousticlanguagemodelevaluati}. Interleaved, duplex-aware decoders. A listening-while-speaking LM fuses a streaming vq-wav2vec encoder with a decoder-only TTS stack via middle fusion at every block, balancing speech quality and duplex responsiveness (WER $\approx$4--4.5%; interactivity F1 up to 98%) \cite{ma2024languagemodelcanlistenwhilespeaking}. SALM-Duplex trains a unified decoder over discrete speech tokens while conditioning on live user encodings for barge-in/overlap; NTPP adds channel embeddings, pair-wise causal masking across channels, and depth-aware RVQ masking so one KV cache serves both sides through overlaps and interruptions \cite{hu2025salmduplexefficientdirectmodelingspeechl,wang2025ntppgenerativespeechlanguagemodelingdual}. Text-initialized stacks interleave short ``plan'' bursts with speech chunks under a single next-token objective to keep caches warm across modalities \cite{zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}.

Section 9 surveys how LM-aware tokenization and conditioning choices shape expressivity, paralinguistics, and style control: LM-aware speech tokenizers (e.g., LAST) align speech units with textual LMs---enabling a single LM to process both speech and text and improving spoken language modeling and ASR---while prosody-aware tokenization (e.g., ProsodyLM's word-level prosody tokens) preserves content--prosody interdependence and yields emergent abilities such as contrastive focus, emotion/stress understanding, and long-context prosody consistency; it further details control interfaces that inject explicit paralinguistic metadata or implicitly generated QA pairs (boosting LLM-judged empathetic reasoning by 38.41%, up to 46.02% when combined), and reviews evaluation practices covering naturalness, intelligibility, style consistency, and LLM-judged metrics validated via correlation with classification measures. \cite{qian2025prosodylmuncoveringemergingprosodyproces,turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,wang2025incorporatingcontextualparalinguisticund}

Section 6 deepens the survey of speech-token language modeling by contrasting autoregressive and non-autoregressive decoding with interleaved text--speech training built from large-scale synthetic text-to-token data that bypass audio generation; it details modality transfer via LM-aware tokenization (e.g., LAST, which uses a text-LM objective to learn discrete speech units so one pre-trained LM can process both speech and text, with trade-offs in speech vocabulary and LM size) and via continued pretraining of a text LM to 1T tokens (including ~600B interleaved speech--text), enabling duplex speech I/O and revealing scaling under token-rate constraints, where ASR-derived VQ tokenizers preserve semantics even at low frame rates (~12.5 Hz) while maintaining reconstruction quality and delivering state-of-the-art spoken LM and QA performance (e.g., improving spoken QA from 13% to 31%). \cite{turetzky2024lastlanguagemodelawarespeechtokenization,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint}

Recent results collectively supply the missing pieces for replacing cascaded ASR$\rightarrow$LLM$\rightarrow$TTS with a single end-to-end SpeechLM: scalable pretraining on 1T tokens built from synthetic interleaved text--speech-token data (including 600B synthetic) that bypasses waveform generation and uses ASR-derived VQ tokenizers to preserve semantics at low frame rates; dual-token SpeechLMs that align with text via an understanding-driven tokenizer while generating acoustic tokens with stabilized training; simple, robust, training-free dMel discretization that supports a unified LM-style architecture for both ASR and TTS; and joint speech--text LMs that enable zero-shot cross-modal transfer and competitive spoken chatbots---evidenced by large gains in spoken QA (e.g., 13% to 31%) and end-to-end dialogue purely in the speech domain. \cite{bai2025dmelspeechtokenizationmadesimple,entry2025towardjointlanguagemodelingspeechunitste,zeng2024scalingspeechtextpretrainingsyntheticint,wang2025dualspeechlmtowardsunifiedspeechundersta}
